starts with \"https://\", so we check both names.\r\n */\r\nfunction hasSessionCookie(request: NextRequest): boolean {\r\n  return !!(\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"better-auth.session_token\")?.value ||\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"__Secure-better-auth.session_token\")?.value\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Get full Better Auth session via API call.\r\n * Only used for admin routes that need the request.cookies.get(\"better-auth.session_token\");\r\n  if (!sessionCookie?.value) return null;\r\n\r\n  try {\r\n    const baseUrl = request.nextUrl.origin;\r\n    const response = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/auth/get-session`, {\r\n      headers: {\r\n        cookie: request.headers.get(\"cookie\") || \"\",\r\n      },\r\n    });\r\n\r\n    if (!response.ok) return null;\r\n\r\n DownloadModalContext\n\"use client\";\n\nimport { createContext, useContext, useState, ReactNode } from \"react\";\n\ninterface DownloadModalContextType {\n  isOpen: boolean;\n  openModal: () => void;\n  closeModal: () => void;\n}\n\nconst DownloadModalContext = createContext<\n  DownloadModalContextType | undefined\n>(undefined);\n\nexport function DownloadModalProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {\n  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);\n\n  const openModal = () => setIsOpen(true);\n  const closeModal = () => setIsOpen(false);\n\n  return (\n    <DownloadModalContext.Provider value={{ isOpen, openModal, closeModal }}>\n      {children}\n    </DownloadModalContext.Provider>\n  );\n}\n\nexport function useDownloadModal() {\n  const context = useContext(DownloadModalContext);\n\n  if (context === undefined) {\n    throw new Error(\n      \"useDownloadModal must be used within a DownloadModalProvider\",\n    );\n  }\n\n  return context;\n}\n=== DownloadModal\n\"use client\";\n\nimport { useState, useEffect } from \"react\";\nimport {\n  Modal,\n  ModalContent,\n  ModalHeader,\n  ModalBody,\n  ModalFooter,\n} from \"@heroui/modal\";\nimport { Button } from \"@heroui/button\";\nimport { Input } from \"@heroui/input\";\nimport Image from \"next/image\";\nimport { useTranslations } from \"next-intl\";\n\nimport { useRouter } from \"@/src/i18n/navigation\";\nimport { useDownloadModal } from \"@/contexts/DownloadModalContext\";\nimport { api } from \"@/lib/trpc/client\";\n\nexport default function DownloadModal() {\n  const { isOpen, closeModal } = useDownloadModal();\n  const t = useTranslations(\"downloadModal\");\n  const router = useRouter();\n  const [email, setEmail] = useState(\"\");\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  const recordDownload = api.download.recordDownload.useMutation({\n    onSuccess: () => {\n      router.push(\"/download\");\n      closeModal();\n    },\n    onError: (err) => {\n      setError(err.message || t(\"errorGeneric\"));\n    },\n  });\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!isOpen) {\n      setEmail(\"\");\n      setError(null);\n    }\n  }, [isOpen]);\n\n  const handleDownload = () => {\n    if (!email) return;\n    setError(null);\n    recordDownload.mutate({ email });\n  };\n\n  const handleSkipAndDownload = () => {\n    setError(null);\n    router.push(\"/download\");\n    closeModal();\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <Modal\n      classNames={{\n        base: \"bg-gray-900 border border-gray-800\",\n        header: \"border-b border-gray-800\",\n        body: \"py-6\",\n        footer: \"border-t border-gray-800\",\n      }}\n      isOpen={isOpen}\n      size=\"lg\"\n      onClose={closeModal}\n    >\n      <ModalContent>\n        <ModalHeader className=\"flex flex-col gap-1 items-center pt-8 pb-4\">\n          {/* App icon */}\n          <div className=\"w-24 h-24 mb-6 relative\">\n            <div className=\"w-full h-full bg-gradient-to-b from-gray-700 to-gray-900 rounded-2xl flex items-center justify-center shadow-2xl\">\n              <Image\n                alt={t(\"logoAlt\")}\n                className=\"w-24 h-24 rounded-xl\"\n                height={96}\n                src=\"/icon/256.png\"\n                width={96}\n              />\n            </div>\n          </div>\n\n          <h3 className=\"text-2xl font-bold text-white mb-2\">{t(\"title\")}</h3>\n        </ModalHeader>\n\n        <ModalBody className=\"py-4 space-y-2 text-center\">\n          <Input\n            classNames={{\n              base: \"w-full\",\n              inputWrapper:\n                \"bg-black border-2 border-blue-500/50 hover:border-blue-500 focus-within:!border-blue-500\",\n              input: \"text-white placeholder:text-gray-500\",\n            }}\n            placeholder={t(\"emailPlaceholder\")}\n            size=\"lg\"\n            type=\"email\"\n            value={email}\n            onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)}\n            onKeyDown={(e) => {\n              if (e.key === \"Enter\" && email) {\n                handleDownload();\n              }\n            }}\n          />\n          {error ? (\n            <p aria-live=\"polite\" className=\"text-sm text-red-500\" role=\"alert\">\n              {error}\n            </p>\n          ) : (\n            <p className=\"text-sm text-gray-400\">{t(\"description\")}</p>\n          )}\n        </ModalBody>\n\n        <ModalFooter className=\"pt-4 pb-6 flex-col gap-3\">\n          <Button\n            fullWidth\n            className=\"bg-gray-200 text-black font-semibold hover:bg-white transition-colors\"\n            isDisabled={!email}\n            isLoading={recordDownload.isPending}\n            size=\"lg\"\n            onClick={handleDownload}\n          >\n            {t(\"downloadButton\")}\n          </Button>\n          <Button\n            fullWidth\n            className=\"text-gray-300 hover:text-white transition-colors\"\n            isDisabled={recordDownload.isPending}\n            size=\"lg\"\n            variant=\"light\"\n            onClick={handleSkipAndDownload}\n          >\n            {t(\"skipAndDownloadButton\")}\n          </Button>\n        </ModalFooter>\n      </ModalContent>\n    </Modal>\n  );\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"9dcd37a6-4983-4f82-95f9-f53379b88bdf","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:46:24.069Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"=== DownloadModalContext\n\"use client\";\n\nimport { default function DownloadPage() {\n  const t = useTranslations(\"downloadPage\");\n  const searchParams = useSearchParams();\n  const [selectedPlatform, setSelectedPlatform] = useState<Platform>(\"mac\");\n  const [downloadState, setDownloadState] = useState<\n    Record<\n      Platform,\n      { url: string | null; countdown: number; started: boolean }\n    >\n  >({\n    mac: { url: null, countdown: 5, started: false },\n    windows: { url: null, countdown: 5, started: false },\n  });\n  const [copied, setCopied] = useState(false);\n\n  const currentState = downloadState[selectedPlatform];\n\n  // Detect user's OS on mount and set platform accordingly\n  // URL query param takes precedence over OS detection\n  useEffect(() => {\n    // Check URL param first\n    const platformParam = searchParams.get(\"platform\");\n\n    if (platformParam === \"windows\") {\n      setSelectedPlatform(\"windows\");\n\n      return;\n    }\n    if (platformParam === \"mac\") {\n      setSelectedPlatform(\"mac\");\n\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // Fall back to OS detection\n    const platform = navigator.platform?.toLowerCase() || \"\";\n    const userAgent = navigator.userAgent?.toLowerCase() || \"\";\n\n    if (platform.includes(\"win\") || userAgent.includes(\"windows\")) {\n      setSelectedPlatform(\"windows\");\n    }\n    // macOS is default, no change needed\n  }, [searchParams]);\n\n  // Fetch the download URL on mount (but don't trigger download yet)\n  // Skip pre-fetching for Windows since we have explicit architecture buttons\n  useEffect(() => {\n    // Windows uses explicit architecture buttons, no need to pre-fetch\n    if (selectedPlatform === \"windows\") return;\n\n    const fetchDownloadUrl = async () => {\n      try {\n        const response = await fetch(\n          `/api/download?platform=${selectedPlatform}`,\n          {\n            redirect: \"follow\",\n          },\n        );\n        const url =\n          response.url || `/api/download?platform=${selectedPlatform}`;\n\n        setDownloadState((prev) => ({\n          ...prev,\n          [selectedPlatform]: { ...prev[selectedPlatform], url },\n        }));\n      } catch (error) {\n        console.error(\"Failed to fetch download URL:\", error);\n        setDownloadState((prev) => ({\n          ...prev,\n          [selectedPlatform]: {\n            ...prev[selectedPlatform],\n            url: `/api/download?platform=${selectedPlatform}`,\n          },\n        }));\n      }\n    };\n\n    // Only fetch if we don't have a URL yet\n    if (!currentState.url) {\n      fetchDownloadUrl();\n    }\n  }, [selectedPlatform, currentState.url]);\n\n  // Reset countdown when switching platforms\n  useEffect(() => {\n    setDownloadState((prev) => ({\n      ...prev,\n      [selectedPlatform]: {\n        ...prev[selectedPlatform],\n        countdown: 5,\n        started: false,\n      },\n    }));\n  }, [selectedPlatform]);\n\n  // Countdown Structural, order-insensitive: enclosure is read *from the same item object* as the os tag.\n- `nextjs/vercel.json:\"buildCommand\"` runs `npm run validate-appcast` → gate is green.\n- `nextjs/app/api/download/route.ts:66-71` runtime: `new RegExp('<item>[\\\\s\\\\S]*?<sparkle:os>windows-${arch}</sparkle:os>[\\\\s\\\\S]*?<enclosure[^>]*url=\"([^\"]+)\"')`. Order-**sensitive**: it walks forward from the *first* `<item>` in the document and takes the first `<enclosure>` appearing **after** the matched `<sparkle:os>` — which may live in a *different* `<item>`.\n\n**seam** `validate-windows-appcast.js` (XML-parser, per-item) ↔ `route.ts:66` (regex, document-linear). The validator guarantees \"every item has a correct enclosure\"; the redirect assumes \"the first enclosure after my os tag belongs to my item.\" Nothing enforces that `<sparkle:os>` precedes `<enclosure>`.\n\n**trigger_sketch** Edit `public/appcast-windows.xml` so each item lists `<enclosure .../>` before `<sparkle:os>` (a plausible hand-edit; the file is hand-maintained per its own header comment), then `GET /api/download?platform=windows&arch=arm64`. I ran this against the real regex:\n\n```\nLIVE        x64   -> .../HyperWhisper-1.8.2-x64-Setup.exe      (correct today)\nLIVE        arm64 -> .../HyperWhisper-1.8.2-arm64-Setup.exe    (correct today)\nREORDERED   arm64 -> https://builds.hyperwhisper.com/X64.exe   <-- WRONG BINARY\nREORDERED   x64   -> null                                      <-- 500\n```\n\nSame class, no reordering needed: if any future item is missing its `<enclosure>`, the arch match falls through into the **next (older) item** and silently serves a stale version.\n\n**consequence** ARM64 Windows users are handed the x64 setup (installs/runs wrong or refuses); x64 users get `{\"error\":\"Failed to get latest download URL\"}` 500. Build stays green. Today's file happens to be ordered correctly, so this is latent-but-armed.\n\n**confidence** high (regex behaviour verified by execution; validator parser confirmed as `xml2js`)\n\n---\n\n### F2 — Welcome-email amplification: no per-recipient dedup anywhere, and the only limiter is keyed on a rotatable IP\n\n**trace** `download.ts:135` `recordDownload` (publicProcedure, no auth, no captcha) → `:144` `getClientIPFromHeaders(ctx.headers)` → `:145` `downloadEmailRateLimiter.limit(clientIP)` (`lib/rate-limit.ts:8`, 10/h, key `ratelimit:download-email:<ip>`) → `:181` `upsertEmail` (`db-layer.ts:887`, `onConflictDoNothing`) → `:213` `emailService.sendWelcomeEmail` → `email.ts:195` `safeLogSentEmail` → `sent_emails`.\n\n**seam** `rate-limit.ts` ↔ `download.ts:145/213`. The limiter's unit of protection is **the sender's IP**; the harm's unit is **the recipient's inbox**. `upsertEmail`'s `onConflictDoNothing` looks like dedup but is applied to a *different* side effect than the email, and it runs on a `try/catch` that swallows failure (`:188-191`). `sent_emails` (`schema/sent-emails.ts`) is explicitly an *append-only audit log* and is **never read** — no code queries it before sending. So there is no dedup authority at all: the same `{email}` sent 10× yields 10 identical welcome emails from `support@hyperwhisper.com`.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n```\nPOST /api/trpc/download.recordDownload   {\"json\":{\"email\":\"victim@example.com\"}}\n```\n×10 per hour per bucket. Bucket rotation needs **no header forgery**: on Vercel `x-vercel-forwarded-for` is the *real* client IP, and any cheap VPS with an IPv6 /64 gives 2^64 distinct legitimate source addresses — the limiter keys on the full address with no /64 collapsing. 10 emails × unlimited buckets.\n\n**consequence** Attacker mailbombs arbitrary third parties from the product's own verified sending domain; Resend reputation/domain burn, spam complaints against `hyperwhisper.com`, unbounded Resend spend. Also unbounded rows in `emails`/`sent_emails`.\n\n**confidence** high for \"no dedup + limiter is per-IP not per-recipient\"; medium-high for the IPv6 rotation being free in practice.\n\n---\n\n### F3 — `getClientIPFromHeaders` trusts `cf-connecting-ip` with no proxy-depth or provenance check; `isIP()` validates *shape*, not *trust* (C7 divergence)\n\n**trace** `download-ip.ts:14-26` candidate order: `x-vercel-forwarded-for` → **`cf-connecting-ip`** → `x-forwarded-for` → `x-real-ip`, each gated only by `isPlainIP` (`:7`, `isIP()` + no `%` zone-id). Feeds `download.ts:145` (rate-limit key), `:174` (`getCountryFromIP`), `:184` (`emails.ip_address`).\nContrast `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/request-id.ts:27-41` `isOffEdgePeer`, which explicitly refuses to honour `Fly-Client-IP`/`X-Forwarded-For` unless the **unforgeable kernel-reported TCP peer address** proves the request transited the edge, and returns `'unknown'` otherwise.\n\n**seam** `download-ip.ts` ↔ `rate-limit.ts`. The limiter assumes its key is an identity; `getClientIPFromHeaders` only guarantees the key is a *syntactically valid IP literal*. `isIP()` answers \"is this a well-formed address\", never \"did this address originate this request\" — the two IP-validation tests pin exactly this format-only contract and nothing about trust:\n- `tests/download-ip-validation.test.ts` → *\"extracts valid IPv4 and IPv6 header candidates\"*, *\"prefers trusted edge headers over raw forwarded headers\"*, *\"skips malformed forwarded values before considering fallback headers\"*, *\"returns unknown when all header candidates are malformed\"*. The third test's fixture (`\"1.1.1.1/../../foo?x=\"`) shows the concern being defended was **path-injection into the proxycheck URL**, not spoofing.\n- `tests/geolocation-ip-validation.test.ts` → *\"rejects malformed IPs before calling proxycheck\"*, *\"encodes valid IP path segment when calling proxycheck\"*. Same: format only.\n\n**trigger_sketch** `POST /api/trpc/download.recordDownload` with `Cf-Connecting-IP: 198.51.100.<n>` and `{\"json\":{\"email\":\"victim@example.com\"}}`, incrementing `n`.\n\n**honest downgrade** On Vercel, `x-vercel-forwarded-for` is set by the platform and `x-vercel-*` is reserved, so candidate[0] normally shadows the forged `cf-connecting-ip` — the header is *untrustworthy but shadowed*. The gap is reachable when `x-vercel-forwarded-for` is absent: local/self-hosted runs, any non-Vercel front door, or a future migration. Note also that Cloudflare is **not** in front of this app (nothing sets `cf-connecting-ip`), so that candidate is pure attacker input with no legitimate producer — it should not be in the list at all. Independently of spoofing, F2's IPv6 rotation already defeats the limiter without touching a header.\n\n**consequence** Rate-limit bypass; poisoned `emails.ip_address`/`country` analytics; wasted proxycheck quota.\n\n**confidence** medium (bypass reachable off-Vercel / trivially via IPv6; header forgery alone likely shadowed on Vercel today)\n\n---\n\n### F4 — The un-timed appcast fetch runs *after* the email is sent, its result is discarded by the only client, and the resulting hang drives a retry that sends a duplicate email\n\n**trace** `download.ts:213` send welcome email → `:225` `const directDownloadUrl = await getLatestDownloadUrl(origin)` → `:87-90` `fetch(\\`${origin}/appcast.xml\\`, { cache: \"no-store\" })` — **no `AbortSignal.timeout`**, unlike the sibling at `app/api/download/route.ts:52` which was deliberately given 3 s with the comment *\"Fail fast instead of holding a serverless function open…\"* → `:230` returns `downloadUrl` → `components/landing/DownloadModal.tsx:28-32` `onSuccess: () => { router.push(\"/download\") }` — **`downloadUrl` is never read**.\n\n**seam** `download.ts:225` (producer) ↔ `DownloadModal.tsx:28` (consumer). The 3 s-timeout lesson was learned and applied in `route.ts` but never propagated back to the tRPC copy of the same helper; and the value it blocks on is dead. Compounded by F2's missing dedup: `onError` (`:31-33`) surfaces an error and leaves the button live, so the natural user reaction (press again) re-runs the whole mutation including a second `sendWelcomeEmail`.\n\n**trigger_sketch** Any slow/hung response from `${NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL}/appcast.xml` (origin CDN incident, Vercel edge stall). User submits email → email is already sent and the row already written → mutation blocks up to the function's max duration → tRPC fails → modal shows an error → user clicks Download again → second identical welcome email, second rate-limit token.\n\n**consequence** User sees \"download failed\" after the download actually succeeded server-side; duplicate welcome emails; serverless functions pinned open at the platform ceiling during an origin incident.\n\n**confidence** high (both the missing timeout and the discarded return value are directly readable)\n\n---\n\n### F5 — tRPC surfaces the raw Zod issue array as `error.message`; the modal renders it verbatim\n\n**trace** `download.ts:137-139` `z.object({ email: z.string().email(\"Invalid email format\") })` → `server/api/trpc.ts:66` `errorFormatter({ shape }) { return shape }` — **no `zodError` flattening**, so tRPC's default input-validation `TRPCError.message` (the `ZodError`'s `message`, i.e. `JSON.stringify(issues, null, 2)`) is what ships → `DownloadModal.tsx:31-33` `setError(err.message || t(\"errorGeneric\"))` → rendered inside `<p role=\"alert\">`.\n\n**seam** `trpc.ts:66` (error contract: \"pass the shape through untouched\") ↔ `DownloadModal.tsx:31` (assumes `message` is human-readable prose, since every *hand-thrown* `TRPCError` in `download.ts` has a friendly string). The zod path is the one error source that doesn't honour that assumption — and it's the single most common error a real user hits.\n\n**trigger_sketch** Open the download modal, type `abc`, press Enter. (`<Input type=\"email\">` does no validation outside a `<form>`; `handleDownload` only checks `if (!email) return`.)\n\n**consequence** The user sees a multi-line JSON blob (`[ { \"validation\": \"email\", \"code\": \"invalid_string\", \"message\": \"Invalid email format\", \"path\": [\"email\"] } ]`) in the red error slot instead of \"Invalid email format\". The custom message was written and is thrown away by the formatter.\n\n**confidence** medium-high\n\n---\n\n### F6 — `/download` page fetches the whole installer (or reliably fails CORS) just to learn the redirect target\n\n**trace** `app/api/download/route.ts:128` `NextResponse.redirect(downloadUrl)` (a cross-origin `builds-cdn.hyperwhisper.com` URL) ← `app/[locale]/download/page.tsx:64-71` `await fetch('/api/download?platform=mac', { redirect: \"follow\" })` then `response.url` → `:73` stored → `:148-150` `window.location.href = url` (a **second** request for the same file).\n\n**seam** `route.ts` (contract: \"I am a 3xx to a large binary on another origin\") ↔ `page.tsx:64` (assumes a redirect can be cheaply followed to read `.url`). Two mutually exclusive bad outcomes, both real:\n- If `builds-cdn` sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (typical for R2/CloudFront): the browser downloads the full ~33 MB DMG into the fetch, the body is never read or cancelled, then `window.location.href` downloads it **again**. 2× egress per visitor.\n- If it does not: the cross-origin redirect fails CORS, `fetch` rejects, `:77` catch sets `url` to the relative `/api/download?...` — so the \"prefetch the URL\" feature has never once worked, and every page view logs a console error.\n\n`route.ts` also implements only `GET`, so a `HEAD` probe (the correct fix) 405s.\n\n**consequence** Doubled CDN bandwidth for every macOS download, or a permanently dead code path plus console noise. `dynamic = \"force-dynamic\"` guarantees a fresh function invocation for each of the two hits.\n\n**confidence** medium (which branch fires depends on the CDN's CORS headers, which I did not probe — both branches are defects)\n\n---\n\n### F7 — Rate limiter fails **closed**: an Upstash outage takes the entire download funnel offline\n\n**trace** `lib/clients/redis.ts:3` `new Redis({ url: process.env.UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL! })` → `rate-limit.ts:8` `new Ratelimit({ redis, limiter: slidingWindow(10,\"1 h\") })` — **no `timeout` and no `ephemeralCache`** option, which are the only ways `@upstash/ratelimit@2` degrades gracefully → `download.ts:145` `await downloadEmailRateLimiter.limit(clientIP)` rejects → falls to the catch at `:232`, is not a `TRPCError`, becomes `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` at `:239`.\n\n**seam** `rate-limit.ts` ↔ `download.ts:141-152`. Every *other* dependency in this mutation is explicitly best-effort — geolocation (`:173-177` \"don't block the download\"), DB write (`:188-191` \"Don't fail the request\"), email send (`:221-223`), appcast (`:103-106` returns null). The limiter is the sole hard dependency, and it's the one third party whose failure has no security consequence. Note also `lib/clients/redis.ts` uses non-null assertions on the env vars, so a missing/expired Upstash credential produces the same total outage rather than a startup error.\n\n**trigger_sketch** Upstash REST endpoint unreachable/401 → every `recordDownload` returns `{\"error\":{\"code\":-32603,\"message\":\"Internal server error\"}}` → the modal shows a generic failure and nobody can get a download link. (Contrast: the `/api/download` route has no limiter at all, so the *unauthenticated, unlimited* path stays up while the email path dies.)\n\n**consequence** Full download-funnel outage on a third-party cache incident.\n\n**confidence** medium-high\n\n---\n\n### F8 — Hostname-only rewrite preserves scheme, port, userinfo, path, query and does not XML-entity-decode\n\n**trace** `route.ts:79-84` and `download.ts:99-102`: `const latestUrl = new URL(match[1]); latestUrl.hostname = \"builds-cdn.hyperwhisper.com\"; return latestUrl.toString()`. `match[1]` is a raw XML attribute value captured by `url=\"([^\"]+)\"` — no entity decoding.\n\n**seam** `validate-appcast.js:41-48` / `validate-windows-appcast.js:141` (build-time invariant: `new URL(u).hostname === \"builds.hyperwhisper.com\"` — **hostname only**, matching the rewrite's blind spot exactly) ↔ the runtime rewrite. Both layers check the same one component and neither checks the other five. Verified behaviour of the rewrite:\n\n```\nhttps://u:p@builds.hyperwhisper.com/a.dmg   -> https://u:p@builds-cdn.hyperwhisper.com/a.dmg   (userinfo survives)\nhttp://builds.hyperwhisper.com:8443/a.dmg   -> http://builds-cdn.hyperwhisper.com:8443/a.dmg   (scheme downgrade + port survive; validator passes both)\njavascript:alert(1)                          -> javascript:alert(1)   (hostname setter is a no-op on opaque-path URLs)\ndata:text/html,<script>x</script>            -> unchanged\n.../a.dmg?a=1&amp;b=2                        -> .../a.dmg?a=1&amp;b=2  (literal \"&amp;\" in the query)\n```\n\n**trigger_sketch** (a) An appcast enclosure written as `http://builds.hyperwhisper.com/...` — the build validator accepts it (it only compares `.hostname`) and users are 307'd to an **HTTP** download of a signed installer. (b) Any enclosure with a query string written correctly as XML (`&amp;`) yields a redirect with a literal `&amp;` — signed-URL/CDN params break and the download 403s. (c) `javascript:`/`data:` require appcast control and are neutered by browsers ignoring non-HTTP `Location`, so they are inert here — reported only to close the question posed.\n\n**Is the appcast trusted?** It is a static file in `nextjs/public/`, so under normal operation yes. But `route.ts:109` derives `origin` from `new URL(request.url).origin`, i.e. from the **Host header** — not from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (which is what `download.ts:119/196` uses). So EP-34's fetch target is request-influenced while EP-47's is env-pinned. On Vercel, edge routing rejects unknown Hosts, so I could not construct a working SSRF and I am **not** claiming one; the honest statement is that the two entry points into the *same helper* disagree about where `origin` comes from, and the trusted-origin assumption at `route.ts:49` is inherited from the request rather than from config.\n\n**consequence** Protocol-downgraded installer download (a); broken/403 download URL (b); an inconsistent trust base for the appcast fetch across EP-34 vs EP-47.\n\n**confidence** medium for (a)/(b) as latent-with-nameable-trigger; low for the Host-derived-origin SSRF (unproven, reported as a divergence not an exploit)\n\n---\n\n### F9 — `/api/download` self-fetches its own origin; that fetch is 401'd on every protected preview deployment\n\n**trace** `route.ts:109` `origin` from the request → `:49-53` `fetch(\\`${origin}/appcast.xml\\`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(3000) })` → `:55` `if (!res.ok) return null` → `:120-124` HTTP 500 `{\"error\":\"Failed to get latest download URL\"}`.\n\n**seam** `route.ts:55`'s error contract (\"`!res.ok` means no release exists\") ↔ Vercel Deployment Protection, which returns a 401 SSO page for the function's own outbound request to itself. `appcast.xml` is a static file already present on disk in the same deployment; nothing requires a network round-trip to read it.\n\n**trigger_sketch** Any preview deploy with Vercel Authentication on (the default): `GET /api/download?platform=mac` → 500. Same shape if the 3 s timeout trips during an origin incident, or on any misconfigured `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` for the tRPC copy.\n\n**consequence** Download button dead on every protected preview (so this path is never exercised in review); in prod, a slow origin turns into a hard 500 rather than a stale-but-working link — there is no last-known-good fallback anywhere.\n\n**confidence** medium-high\n\n---\n\n### F10 — `download.getLatestUrl` (EP-47) has zero callers but remains a public, unrate-limited, un-timed outbound-fetch endpoint\n\n**trace** `download.ts:117-130`. Grep across `nextjs/**/*.{ts,tsx}` finds references only in `server/api/root.ts:8` (a doc comment) and its own definition. `DownloadModal.tsx` uses only `recordDownload`; `download/page.tsx` uses only `/api/download`.\n\n**seam** Dead producer ↔ absent consumer. Because it is dead, its **divergent error contract** has never mattered and has never been noticed: the identical helper returns `null` in `recordDownload` (`:230` falls back to `${origin}/api/download`) but `throws INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` here (`:123-127`). If anyone wires this up, they inherit the strict contract by surprise. Meanwhile it is callable now: unauthenticated, `cache: \"no-store\"`, **no timeout**, no rate limiter, one inbound tRPC call → one outbound origin fetch.\n\n**consequence** Free 1:1 request amplifier against the origin; a latent error-contract trap for the next caller.\n\n**confidence** high (dead code + contract divergence); low on the amplification being materially exploitable\n\n---\n\n### F11 — Disposable-domain gate fails **open**, and its cache layer is vestigial (checked; not a race)\n\n**trace** `download.ts:33-34` module globals `cachedDisposableDomains`/`lastLoadMs` → `:41-56` `getDisposableDomains()` → `lib/disposable_domains.ts:1` `export const disposableDomains: string[]` — a **static, statically-imported array**, not a fetch.\n\n**assessment, as asked:** there is **no** race and **no** load-failure path. `getDisposableDomains` is synchronous work in an `async` wrapper; two concurrent requests can both rebuild the Set, but both produce identical values and the last write wins harmlessly. It can never cache an empty set because the source can never fail. `lastLoadMs` cannot wedge — worst case it re-`map`/`filter`s ~5 000 strings once an hour per instance. The `async` signature, the 1-hour TTL and the cache are all dead weight around a compile-time constant, which is why `isDisposableDomain:65` opens with `if (list.size === 0) return false` — a **fail-open** guard written for a runtime-loaded list that no longer exists. If anyone converts `disposable_domains.ts` to a network/DB load (the shape the cache was clearly written for), that one line silently disables the entire gate on the first fetch failure, for a full hour, per instance, with no log line. Reporting as a live fail-open contract, not a current bug.\n\n**confidence** high that it is *not* currently a race/empty-cache bug; medium that the fail-open guard is a real trap\n\n---\n\n### F12 — Unvalidated `user-agent` → DB: no 500, but no bound either (checked)\n\n**trace** `download.ts:185` `ctx.headers.get(\"user-agent\") || null` → `db-layer.ts:887` `upsertEmail` → `schema/emails.ts:8` `userAgent: text(\"user_agent\")`.\n\n**assessment, as asked:** `text` is unbounded in Postgres and `user_agent` carries no index, so an oversized UA neither 500s nor truncates — it is stored whole. Invalid UTF-8 cannot arrive (the `Headers` API yields a JS string). The only genuinely bounded column in the path is `emails.email`, which is `.unique()` (btree, ~2 704-byte limit) — and `z.string().email()` at `:138` has **no `.max()`**, so a several-KB address that satisfies the email regex reaches the insert and errors with *\"index row size exceeds maximum for btree\"*. That is caught and swallowed at `:188-191` (\"Don't fail the request if email storage fails\"), so the request proceeds and **still sends** the welcome email to the oversized address, which Resend then rejects as a non-retryable error (`email.ts:213`). Net effect: a silent no-op with three log lines, degrading correctly. Reported as a boundary mismatch (`z.email()` unbounded ↔ btree bounded) that is currently absorbed rather than a live failure.\n\n**confidence** medium (no user-visible break; the seam is real but the swallow saves it)\n\n---\n\n### F13 — proxycheck integration: non-200/malformed/rate-limited all collapse to `null`; key is never logged, but the response-scan fallback can attribute the wrong country\n\n**trace** `lib/services/geolocation.ts:47-65`. Answering the questions posed: a non-200 → `:57` `return null`; a 429/quota-exhausted body → `:57` or `:61` `?? null`; malformed JSON → the bare `catch` at `:62` → `null`; a 3 s timeout → same. A third-party outage therefore **never blocks downloads and never throws** — `download.ts:173-177` wraps it in a second try/catch it does not need. The key is interpolated into the request URL at `:54` but that URL is never logged and the catch is bare (`catch {}`, no error object), so `PROXYCHECK_API_KEY` cannot reach a log line or the client from this file. `next.config.mjs` sets no `logging.fetches.fullUrl`, so Next's fetch logger will not print it either. **Clean.**\n\nThe one real seam: `lookupEntry:33-37`'s fallback scans **every** value in the response for the first object containing a `location` key, on the documented assumption that \"the response only ever contains one looked-up address\". That assumption is proxycheck's, not ours, and it is unversioned — if a v3 response ever carries a second `location`-bearing object (a nested `proxy`/`asn`/`network` block, or a batch echo), a download from IP A can be recorded with IP B's country. Silent, unfalsifiable from the data, and only affects social-proof display.\n\n**confidence** low-medium (fallback misattribution is contingent on an upstream shape change; the error-contract and key-leak questions are answered clean)\n\n---\n\n### depth_limits\n\n- **Not executed:** no requests were made to production, proxycheck, Resend or Upstash. F1's regex behaviour was verified by running the exact `RegExp` from `route.ts:66` against the real `public/appcast-windows.xml` and a synthetic reordered fixture; F8's rewrite table was produced by running the exact `new URL(...)` + `hostname=` sequence. Everything else is read-only inference.\n- **`bun test tests` not run** — the two named tests are `node:test` files (`import test from \"node:test\"`); I read and cite them rather than executing, since running them adds nothing to what the assertions already pin.\n- **Vercel platform behaviour asserted from documentation, not observed**: that `x-vercel-forwarded-for` is always set and `x-vercel-*` is reserved (this is what downgrades F3), and that the edge rejects unknown `Host` values (this is what downgrades F8's SSRF leg). Both should be confirmed against the actual project settings before acting on the confidence levels I assigned.\n- **Not traced:** the `builds-cdn.hyperwhisper.com` CORS/response headers (decides which branch of F6 is live); Upstash's configured failure mode at the account level; whether `emails.email` collation/index specifics change F12's threshold; `hyperwhisper-cloud`'s callers of `getClientIP` beyond `request-id.ts` itself (I read the C7 counterpart only for the divergence comparison, not its downstream quota/`ip_blocked` enforcement).\n- **Out of scope by instruction and untouched:** Swift/desktop clients, `.env` contents, any non-TypeScript surface.\n\n###
